Let's focus on robust implementation. We need produce code that likely gets score <1.5027 within time. We can combine:

Let's design local optimizer in detail.

Normalization and scoring:
Given a (np.float64 length n), nonnegative finite.
S = a.sum(); if S<=0 invalid. We'll often normalize a=S=1 for optimization. The objective score = 2n * max(c) / S^2. If normalized sum=1, score=2n max(c). Since c coefficients scale with S^2. If we update and normalize back, max changes.

Compute convolution c = a*a (linear). For speed, use FFT length M_conv = 2n-1 next fast maybe power of two. `np.fft.rfft(a, size=M)`, `C=np.fft.irfft(A*A, size=M)[:2*n-1]`. Multiplication of complex spectra gives linear convolution because zero-padded. Since a real, result nonnegative with small negative due to roundoff; clip c=np.maximum(c,0). This c may differ from np.convolve by roundoff but good. Actual final evaluate uses exact? We can also call evaluate_sequence to get authoritative score for candidates; but it is O(n^2) quite slow for n large (e.g., 6121 ->37M ops; maybe okay 1-2s). For local many iterations, use FFT.

Gradient via weights p (length L=2n-1), normalized sum1 maybe. Need compute g = 2*r, r_m=∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a_j p_{m+j}. Use FFT length M_grad maybe next power of two ≥ n+L-1 =4n-2. But we can avoid full FFT by computing if p has simple form (prefix uniform). For general p from softmax, FFT required.

Method A: Multiplicative gradient with p=softmax β(c) or uniform top K.
- p normalized sum=1. gradient of logsumexp if p normalized: g = 2*corr(a,p) (with p normalized). Actually derivative of log∑exp(c) = normalized weights p. If using L=logsumexp(c) (τ=1), g=2corr(a,p). If using β, p=exp(β c)/Z, gradient of (1/β)logsumexp(βc) = pcorr; top. We can set p top.
- Update log-weights z: z = z - η * g (or with Adam). Then a = softmax(z). Since objective invariant to scaling, sum fixed. Need ensure not too many iterations.

But `a = softmax(z)` can be computed stably: z -= max(z); e=exp(z); a=e/e.sum(). This makes a positive tiny. For n large, underflow zeros possible; okay if total mass not lost. Alternatively multiplicative update directly: a_new = a * exp(-η g); then normalize. This avoids maintaining z and may be faster. But repeated multiplicative updates can underflow early elements. Use z = log(a)+const. We can maintain z; after g, z -= lr * g; periodically center z mean 0; compute a from z. This is stable.

Gradient scale: For normalized a and p sum1, g components maybe ~O(1/n) to O(1/√n)? For square-root, g0~? n=4096, g0 ~ 4/√n=0.06? Let's estimate: a~1/(S√π k)~1/(2√n * √π k?) Algorithm:
```
def optimize_sequence(n, seed, time_budget, initial_kind):
    a = initial(n)
    S = a.sum(); a=some normalization? We'll maintain z log a.
    z = log(a + eps) - mean(log(a+eps))
    current_a = softmax(z)
    current_score = score_fft(current_a)
    beta_q schedule? maybe q start 4, increase to 32.
    eta = 0.5
    stagnation counter
    while time < deadline:
       c = conv_fft(a)
       cpos = np.maximum(c, 0)
       # choose weights
       # avoid zeros: c_floor = max(c)*1e-12? p = cpos**q
       # if all zero, uniform.
       p = cpos ** q ; psum = p.sum(); if psum=0 uniform.
       p /= psum
       g = gradient_corr(a,p)
       gmax = g.max(); if gmax<=eps: break
       d = g / gmax
       # maybe smooth d? not necessary.
       # try etas
       best_local = None
       for eta in candidate_list:
           zc = z - eta*d
           zc = zc - zc.mean()
           ac = softmax(zc)
           sc = score_fft(ac)
           if sc < best_trial_score: keep
       if best_trial_score < current_score - tol:
           z=best_z; a=best_a; current_score=...
           improvement count
       else:
           # no improvement: reduce q? or allow random restart/modification
```

Let's define:
```
def conv_full_fft(a, size=None):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1; M=1
    while M < L: M <<=1
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, M)[:L]
    return c
```

Softmax from z:
```
def softmax_z(z):
    z = z - z.max()
    ex = np.exp(z)
    s = ex.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return np.full(n,1/n)
    return ex/s
```
Initializing z from raw sqrt sequence: `z = np.log(a) - np.mean(np.log(a))`. But a early all ~1? Raw r0=1, many ~0.008; log okay. Normalize sum via softmax not same as a/H? Wait if z=log(a)+const, softmax gives a_i / sum(a) exactly, yes.

Score function local:
```
def local_score(a, conv_size=None):
    S = a.sum()
    if S<=1e-15: return np.inf
    an = a/S
    c = conv_full_fft(an)
    maxc = c.max()
    if maxc <0: maxc=0.0
    return float(2*a.size*maxc)
```
Since normalized sum1. If a already normalized, skip divide. We maintain normalized. So `c=conv_fft(a)`, score=2n max(c).

Pseudo:
```
eta = 0.5
skip_count=0
while time:
  c=conv(a); score=score_from(c)
  p=weights(c)
  g=grad; d=g/max(g)
  # backtrack
  step=eta
  found=False
  for _ in range(6):
     zc = z - step*d; zc-=mean; ac=softmax(zc); sc=score(ac)
     if sc < score - 1e-12:
         # try step*2 once
         if step < 1.5:
             zc2=zc_base - 2*step*d... maybe
             sc2=score
             if sc2<sc: use step*2
         accept; new_eta=min(2, step*1.2 if good else step); break
     step *= 0.5
  if not found:
     # try random perturb or change weights/n
     step = 0.1? add noise
```

Let's design in detail final code structure:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def _next_p2(m): ...
def _softmax(z): ...
def _sqrt_initial(n): ...
def _conv_fft(a, size): ...
def _local_score_from_c(c,n): ...

`_sqrt_initial(n)`:
```
a = np.empty(n)
a[0]=1.0
for k in range(1,n): a[k]=a[k-1]*(2*k-1)/(2*k)
# maybe scale to sum around sqrt(2n)? no, softmax does.
return a
```
This raw has first coefficient ~1; normalize via z. For log, if a[0]=1, logs fine. For n large, a[i] ~0.008; log ~-4.8; stable.

`_softmax(z)`:
```
z = np.asarray(z, dtype=float)
z = z - z.max()
ex = np.exp(z)
s = ex.sum()
if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0:
   # fallback uniform with positive small
   out=np.full(z.shape, 1.0/z.size)
   return out
return ex/s
```
If z has very negative, exp underflow okay; if all underflow due max? max set to 0, at least max exp=1.

`_conv_fft(a, size)`:
```
A=np.fft.rfft(a, size)
c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, size)
c = c[:2*a.size-1]
# clean small negative
# maybe if c.max()<0 due all zero: c=np.maximum(c,0)
return c
```
Potential issue: `A*A` can produce imaginary? No, but `irfft` ignores imaginary part? It expects complex. Good.

For local score, need `cmax = np.max(c)`. If cmax<=0 return 0. Because a positive, cmax positive. Return 2*n*cmax because a normalized sum1. But if due roundoff cmax negative tiny? okay.

`_gradient(a,p,conv_size,grad_size)`: p length 2n-1 normalized sum1. We pass grad_size. Need ensure p length L. 
```
rev=p[::-1]
A=np.fft.rfft(a, grad_size)
P=np.fft.rfft(rev, grad_size)
conv=np.fft.irfft(A*P, grad_size)
L=len(p); n=len(a)
r=conv[L-1 : L-1-n : -1]
g=2.0*r
# Clean negative small? gradient should nonnegative; due roundoff maybe negative tiny. For safe, g=np.maximum(g,0)
return g
```
Let's test for n=1: L=1, grad_size ≥1-? n+L-1=1, OK. r=conv[0:0:-1]? For n=1, L-1=0, stop=-1, step -1 gives index0? Python conv[0:-1:-1] gives [conv[0]]? Let's check: start 0, stop -1, step -1: includes start then decreasing until > stop; stop -1 not included (index -1 last?), Actually for length1, indices [0], stop=-1 means stop at -1 (before -1?); It returns [0]? Could be empty? Let's not rely; n≥1024. Fine.

`_power_weights(c, q)`:
```
c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
cmax = c.max()
if cmax <=0: return np.full(len(c), 1/len(c))
scaled = c / cmax
# For numerical, set scaled = np.clip(scaled,0,None)
w = scaled ** q
s = w.sum()
if not finite or s<=0:
   idx=np.argmax(c); out=np.zeros_like(c); out[idx]=1; return out
w /= s
return w
```
If q integer; using `scaled ** q` efficient. For q maybe float? We'll use int.

Optimizer:
```
def _optimize(a_init, q, eta, max_time, rng, label, best_holder):
    n = a_init.size
    # normalize and z
    a0 = np.clip(a_init, 0, 1000)
    s0 = a0.sum()
    if s0 <=0: return initial fallback
    a = a0 / s0
    # ensure positive for log: add tiny? If some zeros, log -inf. Maybe use z = log(a+eps) with eps=1e-30? But softmax would make tiny.
    eps = np.finfo(float).tiny
    z = np.log(np.maximum(a, eps))
    z -= z.mean()
    a = _softmax(z)
    conv_size = next_p2(2*n-1)
    grad_size = next_p2(4*n-2)
    c = _conv_fft(a, conv_size)
    score = 2*n*c.max()
    start=time.monotonic()
    improvements=0
    fail_count=0
    step=eta
    best_local=score; best_a=a.copy(); best_z=z.copy()
    while time.monotonic() < start+max_time:
       # update weights maybe q adapt? keep fixed.
       p = _power_weights(c, q)
       g = _gradient(a,p,conv_size,grad_size)
       gmax = g.max()
       if not finite or gmax<=0: break
       d = g / gmax
       # try current step
       z_trial = z - step*d
       z_trial -= z_trial.mean()
       a_trial = _softmax(z_trial)
       c_trial = _conv_fft(a_trial, conv_size)
       s_trial = 2*n*c_trial.max()
       if s_trial < score - 1e-12:
           # maybe try larger step if score improved
           if step < 2.0:
              z2 = z - 2*step*d; z2-=z2.mean(); a2=_softmax(z2); c2=_conv_fft(a2,conv_size); s2=2*n*c2.max()
              if s2 < s_trial:
                  z_trial=a2... step = 2*step? Accept step2
           accept.
           if s_trial < best_local - tol: update best
           improvements++
           fail=0
           step = min(2.0, step*1.1)
       else:
           # reduce step
           if step > 0.02:
               step *=0.6
               fail++ (without scoring smaller? We could just reduce and loop; but next iteration recomputes g from current, not trial. That's okay but wastes direction?                # Maybe change q? not inside fixed q

```

Euclidean projected gradient:
- Direction d = g/gmax (nonnegative). Candidate x = a - η d. Project to simplex. Since d small in early, x for early negative; projection will drop them and distribute. This may transfer mass more aggressively than softmax. Projection algorithm:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.searchsorted(cssv, (cssv[-1]+1)/?) # standard
```
Standard for sum=1:
```
u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
cssv = np.cumsum(u)
cond = (np.arange(1, v.size+1) + (cssv[-1] - 1)?) / etc
```
Better:
```
u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
cum = np.cumsum(u)
n = len(v)
# find largest rho s.t. u_rho + (1 - cum_{rho-1})/rho > 0
cond = u > (cum - 1)/np.arange(1,n+1)
rho = np.max(np.where(cond)[0]) + 1
theta = (cum[rho-1] - 1)/rho
return np.maximum(v - theta, 0)
```
This is O(n log n). Or use `np.partition` for O(n). For n=4096, sorting okay. We can try Euclidean step in parallel. Direction d has components <=1, a components ~1/n. If η ~0.1, x early ~a-0.1 maybe very negative; projection sets early zero, mass to later. This may be too aggressive but line search can accept only if score improves. Could be powerful.

Softmax relative change may be slower. Let's include Euclidean projected candidate generation as an alternative. For each iteration, compute both softmax candidate and Euclidean candidate with their own step? Or run optimizer with mode. Keep best. But time budget.

def _next_power_of_two(n):
    return 1 << (int(n) - 1).bit_length()

3. `_softmax_target(z, target_sum)`:
Need use np. If z contains inf? Ensure finite. If not finite, fallback.
```
def _softmax_target(z, target_sum):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
       idx = np.argmax(z)
       out = np.zeros_like(z); out[idx]=target_sum; return out
    zm = z - np.max(z)
    ex = np.exp(zm)
    s = ex.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       out=np.full(z.shape, target_sum/z.size); return out
    return target_sum * ex / s
```
If all ex underflow? max exp=1 so s≥1.

Need `_local_score(a, conv_size=None)`: normalize to target? If `a` unnormalized, we can just scale target and compute. Let's implement:
```
def _local_score(a, target_sum=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    a = np.clip(a,0,None)
    S=a.sum()
    if S<=... return inf
    if target_sum is None: target_sum=np.sqrt(2*n)
    a = a * (target_sum/S)
    csize=_next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    c=_conv_fft(a,csize)
    cmax=max(c)
    S2=a.sum()*a.sum() # or target_sum^2 if normalized
    return float(2*n*cmax/(S2)) if S2>0 else inf
```
We normalize to target sum, but S after scaling may not exactly; use S2.

`_conv_fft` expects size maybe compute if None.

def _optimize_sequence(a_init, max_seconds, rng, label=''):
    a = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim !=1 or a.size==0: return fallback
    a = np.clip(a, 0, 1000)
    n=a.size
    target=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    S=a.sum()
    if S<=1e-12: a=np.full(n,target/n)
    else: a=a*(target/S)
    # z from a
    z=np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    z -= np.mean(z)
    a=_softmax_target(z,target)
    csize=_next...; gsize=...
    c=_conv_fft(a,csize)
    S2=a.sum()*a.sum(); score=2*n*c.max()/S2
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_z=z.copy()
    start=time.monotonic(); remain=max_seconds
    phases=[(2,0.25),(8,0.45),(32,0.30)]
    if max_seconds<20: phases=[(8,1.0)]
    for q, frac in phases:
       phase_time = max(2.0, max_seconds*frac) # but total maybe > max? Use while time and q changes? Simpler: phase_end = start + max_seconds*frac? Need cumulative.
```

Pseudo:
```
def _optimize_fixed_q(a_init, max_seconds, q, rng, label):
   a, z, c, score = normalize init
   best_a, best_score, best_z = a,z,c? keep best z? Need to reset to best z? Store best_z.
   step=0.4
   fail=0
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max_seconds
   while time<deadline:
       p=_power_weights(c,q)
       g=_gradient(a,p,gsize)
       gmax=g.max()
       if fail>60 or not finite:
           # reset to best with noise
           z=best_z.copy(); z += 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n); z-=z.mean(); a=_softmax_target(z,target); c=_conv_fft(a,csize); score=...; step=0.4; fail=0; fail=100? continue
       d=g/gmax
       # line search steps
       best_candidate_score=score
       best_candidate=None
       for st_factor in (0.5,1.0,2.0):
           st = step*st_factor
           if st <= 0: continue
           zt = z - st*d
           # optional noise if fail>15 and factor==1?
           if fail>15 and rng.random()<0.2:
               zt += 0.02*rng.standard_normal(n)
           zt -= zt.mean()
           at=_softmax_target(zt,target)
           ct=_conv_fft(at,csize)
           st_score=2*n*ct.max()/(at.sum()*at.sum())
           if st_score < best_candidate_score:
               best_candidate_score=st_score; best_candidate=(zt,at,ct)
       if best_candidate is not None and best_candidate_score < score - 1e-12:
           z,a,c=best_candidate; score=...
           step = min(2.0, step*1.1) # maybe chosen? We don't know factor; simple increase if any.
           fail=0
           if score < best_score:
               best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); best_z=z.copy(); best_c? not need
       else:
           step *= 0.7
           if step < 0.02:
               step=0.4; fail = 100 # force reset
           fail +=1
   return best_a, best_score
```

Now, let's consider possible failures:
- `_conv_fft` using `A*A` for a real sequence can have a numerical imaginary part? `irfft` assumes input Hermitian; A*A exactly Hermitian? For real A? A is rfft output length M/2+1. Multiplication A*A: for index k, M-k not present in rfft? irfft uses symmetry to reconstruct, expects value at k and conjugate at M-k for k>0. For k=0 and Nyquist real. A*A at k may not exactly have conjugate relation because A[k]^2 not necessarily magnitude? The full spectrum of convolution is A_full[k]*A_full[k], with A_full[M-k]=conj(A_full[k]), so product[M-k]=conj(A_full[k])^2=conj(product[k]). But rfft only stores k=0..M/2. In product stored, for k>0, irfft assumes the hidden M-k part is conjugate of stored; this is consistent if we treat stored product[k] as complex; irfft will use conjugate for mirror. It doesn't require product[Nyquist] real? It may take real part. Good. The imaginary part of stored product is okay? For irfft, input is complex with Hermitian symmetry implicitly; hidden mirror is conj(input[k]). So if input[k] has imaginary, the inverse real. Fine.
- For gradient, `A*P` with P=FFT(rev_p) real p, same property? A_full*P_full has Hermitian? A_full conjugate, P_full conjugate => product at -k=conj(product k). Good.

- Slice for gradient: verify with p length L. Need ensure `grad_size` large enough for full convolution of a (n) and rev_p (L) length n+L-1=4n-2. We use next_p2(4*n-2). For n=1, get 1? 4-2=2? `next_power_of_two(2)`? Actually n=1 => L=1, need length 1; 4n-2=2, next 2. okay.
- In `_gradient`, p might not have length L exactly if c trimmed? We pass p length L. Good.

Potential performance: `_power_weights` `scaled ** q` for q=32 length16k, okay. `_conv_fft` each candidate uses `np.fft.rfft` and `irfft`. Could optimize by reusing rfft of p? p changes each iteration. Okay.

Alternative compute gradient direct for top active if K small. For q high p may be sparse; direct loops might be faster? But FFT robust. We can add if q>20: find support of p where p>1e-12; if support small (<=512), compute gradient via direct slices without 4n FFT. But code complexity; maybe not needed.

However for n=8192, gradient FFT 32768 is more expensive than conv 16384. Iter cost dominated by 3 candidate convs + gradient. Could reduce line search candidates to 2 (0.5,1.0) and maybe half. For speed, use 2 candidates? If step fixed, but line search robust. We can use 2: factors (0.7,1.0,1.4)? 3 is okay. 400s enough.

Detailed code components:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _next_p2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

def _softmax_target(z, target_sum):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    if z.size == 0: return np.array([], dtype=float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
       idx = int(np.nanmax(z)) if np.all(np.isnan(z))? robust.
       out = np.zeros(z.shape)
       out[idx] = target_sum
       return out
    z = z - np.max(z)
    ex = np.exp(z)
    s = ex.sum(dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
       out = np.full(z.shape, target_sum / z.size, dtype=np.float64)
       return out
    return target_sum * ex / s
```
`np.nanmax` if all nan? z should finite. Simpler:
```
if not finite:
   z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, -1e300)
```

`_make_sqrt(n)`:
```
a=np.empty(n,float)
a[0]=1.0
for i in range(1,n): a[i] = a[i-1]*((2*i-1)/(2*i))
return a
```
For n=0? not.

`_local_score(a)`:
```
def _local_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000.0)
    n=a.size
    S=a.sum(dtype=float)
    if S <= 1e-12: return np.inf
    target=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=a*(target/S)
    csize=_next_p2(2*n-1)
    c=_conv_fft(a,csize)
    cmax=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    S2=float(a.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    S2=S2*S2
    if S2 <=0: return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*cmax/S2)
```
Could use target_sum*target_sum for S2 if normalized exactly, but use actual.

`_choose_best_initial(n, rng, num_best=1)`:

def _choose_best_initial(n, rng):
    cands=_make_initial_candidates(n,rng)
    best=None; best_score=inf
    counts=0
    for cand in cands:
        sc=_local_score(cand)
        if sc<best_score:
           best_score=sc; best=cand.copy()
    # maybe return best
    return best,best_score
```

       return None

    return arr

`_conv_fft(a, size)`:
```
def _conv_fft(a, size):
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    C=A*A
    out=np.fft.irfft(C, size)
    n=a.size
    return out[:2*n-1]
```
Maybe set `C.imag`? no.

def _gradient(a,p,conv_size,grad_size):

    A=np.fft.rfft(a, grad_size)
    P=np.fft.rfft(rev, grad_size)

    L=p.size
    # r = conv[L-1 : L-1-n : -1]
    start=L-1
    stop=start-n
    r=conv[start:stop:-1]
    if r.size != n:
       # fallback slice
       r=conv[L-1-n+1:L].Flipping? Let's verify.
```

`_power_weights(c,q)`:
```
def _power_weights(c,q):
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    cmax=float(np.max(c))
    L=c.size
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <=0:
       return np.full(L,1.0/L)
    scaled=c/cmax
    w=scaled ** int(q)
    ws=w.sum(dtype=float)
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <=0:
       idx=int(np.argmax(c)); w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0; return w
    w /= ws
    return w
```
If q=0? not; maybe if q=0 returns uniform? not.

`_score_from_c(a,c)`:
```
def _score_from_c(a,c):
    S=float(a.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    S2=S*S
    if S2 <=0: return np.inf
    cmax=float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0*a.size*max(0.0,cmax)/S2)
```

`_normalize_for_z(a,target)`:
```
S=a.sum()
if S<=0: a=np.full(n,target/n)
else: a=a*(target/S)
z=np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
z -= z.mean()
return z, _softmax_target(z,target)
```
If a has zeros, log eps; z large negative; softmax creates tiny positive maybe not exactly zero. Fine.

`_optimize_fixed_q(a_init, max_seconds, q, rng, label)`:
Need print and robust. Use `time.monotonic()`.
Parameters: q may be float? use int in weights.
```
def _optimize_fixed_q(a_init, max_seconds, q, rng, label=''):
    a = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim !=1 or a.size ==0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.ones(max(1, int(a.size if a.size else 256)), dtype=float)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n=a.size
    if n < 1: return None,np.inf
    target=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    S=a.sum()
    if S <= 1e-12:
        a=np.full(n,target/n)
    else:
        a=a*(target/S)
    z=np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300)); z-=z.mean()
    a=_softmax_target(z,target)
    csize=_next_p2(2*n-1); gsize=_next_p2(4*n-2)
    c=_conv_fft(a,csize); score=_score_from_c(a,c)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_z=z.copy()
    if max_seconds <=0: return best_a,best_score
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max_seconds
    step=0.4
    fail=0
    iter_count=0
    last_print=0.0
    try:
      print(f"[{label}] n={n} q={q} init={score:.9f}")
    except Exception: pass
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # If too close to deadline, break before candidate line search maybe
       cpos=np.maximum(c,0.0)
       p=_power_weights(c,q)
       a_fft? gradient
       g=_gradient(a,p,csize,gsize)
       gmax=float(np.max(g)) if g.size else 0.0
       iter_count +=1
       if iter_count % 2000 ==0:
          print(f"[{label}] iter={iter_count} score={score:.9f} best={best_score:.9f} step={step:.3f} q={q}")
       if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-300:
          break
       d=g/gmax
       # If fail high, reset to best with noise
       if fail > 40:
          noise = 0.05 * min(1.0, fail/100.0) + 0.02
          z = best_z.copy()
          z += noise * rng.standard_normal(n)
          z -= z.mean()
          a = _softmax_target(z,target)
          c = _conv_fft(a,csize); score=_score_from_c(a,c)
          step=0.4
          fail=0
          if score >= best_score:
             # keep best? but we want exploration; still continue
             pass
          continue
       # line search
       best_cand_score=score
       best_cand=None
       chosen_st=step
       for factor in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
          st = step*factor
          if st <= 0: continue
          zt = z - st*d
          if fail > 10 and factor == 1.0 and rng.random() < 0.15:
             zt += 0.02 * rng.standard_normal(n)
          zt -= zt.mean()
          at = _softmax_target(zt,target)
          ct = _conv_fft(at,csize)
          sc = _score_from_c(at,ct)
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score:
             best_cand_score=sc; best_cand=(zt,at,ct); chosen_st=st
       if best_cand is not None:
          z,a,c = best_cand
          score=best_cand_score
          step=min(2.0, max(0.02, chosen_st*1.1))
          fail=0
          if score < best_score:
              best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); best_z=z.copy()
              # optional print if improvement significant
              try:
                 if time.monotonic()-last_print > 5.0:
                    print(f"[{label}] improved best={score:.9f}")
                    last_print=time.monotonic()
                 else:
                    pass
              except Exception: pass
       else:
          step *= 0.7
          fail +=1
          if step < 0.015:
             step=0.4
       # Hard deadline check maybe after long candidate; while condition next
    return best_a, best_score
```

Calling multiple q phases:
```
def _run_job(a_init, max_seconds, q_schedule, rng, label, global_deadline):
   seq=a_init
   score=inf
   for q, frac in q_schedule:
      t = min(max_seconds*frac, global_deadline - time.monotonic())
      if t <=0: break
      seq, score = _optimize_fixed_q(seq, t, q, rng, label+f' q{q}')
   return seq, score
```

    score = np.inf

       now=time.monotonic()
       t = min(t, hard_deadline - now)
       if t <= 2.0:
           continue
       seq, score = _optimize_fixed_q(seq, t, int(q), rng, label)
    return seq, score

Implementation:
```
def _center_clip_z(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    z = z - np.mean(z)
    mn = float(np.min(z)); mx=float(np.max(z))
    rng = mx-mn
    if np.isfinite(rng) and rng > 60.0:
       z = z * (60.0/rng)
    elif not np.isfinite(rng):
       z = np.zeros_like(z)
    return z
```
If z has nan, mean nan; not. We can if not finite, replace.

```
def _make_initial_candidates(n, rng):
    cands=[]
    if n <=0: return [np.ones(1)]
    r=_make_sqrt(n)
    cands.append(r)
    if n>1:
       ratio=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)/(n-1)
       cands.append(r*(1.0+0.3*ratio))
       cands.append(r*(1.0-0.2*ratio))
    else: cands.append(r)
    cands.append(r[::-1].copy())
    for s in (0.5, 0.45):
       cands.append(np.power(np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)+0.5, -s))
    # add slight random modulation
    cands.append(r * np.exp(0.2 * rng.standard_normal(n)))
    return cands
```
For s=0.5, this is similar to r but no binomial coefficients; may have different shape. Good.

Could include `np.power(np.arange(n)+0.5, -0.52)` slightly more singular? Not.

```
def _choose_best_initial(n, rng, k=1):
    cands=_make_initial_candidates(n,rng)
    best=None; best_score=np.inf
    for cand in cands:
       sc=_local_score(cand)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<best_score:
          best_score=sc; best=cand.copy()
    if best is None:
       best=_make_sqrt(n); best_score=_local_score(best)
    return best,best_score
```

```
def _run_job(seq, max_seconds, q_schedule, rng, label, hard_deadline, baseline_score):
    best_seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_score = baseline_score
    for q, frac in q_schedule:
       t = min(max_seconds*frac, hard_deadline - time.monotonic())
       if t <= 1.0: continue
       cur_seq, cur_score = _optimize_fixed_q(best_seq, t, int(q), rng, label)
       if np.isfinite(cur_score) and cur_score < best_score:
           best_score=cur_score; best_seq=cur_seq.copy()
       else:
           # If cur_score not finite but best_seq finite, keep. If cur_seq maybe better despite score inf? no.
           pass
    return best_seq, best_score
```

This is safe (less exploration across phases but each phase fresh from best with noise internal). Good. If phase had no improvement but found a different local with same score? Not kept; maybe okay.

```python
"""
Mirror-descent / active-set local search for nonnegative step heights.

The objective is scale-invariant:
    2*n*max(conv(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

This program:
  * initializes from the formal square-root coefficients of (1-x)**(-1/2),
    which already give scores near pi/2, and from the supplied stored
    sequence if it exists and looks efficient to evaluate;
  * optimizes log-heights with mirror descent;
  * uses FFT-based convolution and convolution-gradient evaluations;
  * mixes a peak-subgradient phase with a flattening L2 phase;
  * keeps the best sequence found by local score.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _next_p2(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _center_clip_z(z):
    """Center logits and prevent excessive dynamic range."""
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    if z.size == 0:
        return z
    z = z - np.mean(z)
    mn = float(np.min(z))
    mx = float(np.max(z))
    rng = mx - mn
    if not np.isfinite(rng):
        return np.zeros_like(z)
    if rng > 60.0:
        z = z * (60.0 / rng)
    return z


def _softmax_target(z, target_sum):
    """Stable softmax scaled to sum to target_sum."""
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    if z.size == 0:
        return z
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, -1e300)
    z = z - np.max(z)
    ex = np.exp(z)
    s = float(ex.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(z.shape, target_sum / z.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return target_sum * ex / s


def _make_sqrt_series(n):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-x)**(-1/2):
    r_k = binom(2k, k) / 4**k.

    Their infinite convolution is 1 + x + x^2 + ..., so truncated versions
    give low peak-convolution sequences.
    """
    a = np.empty(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, a.size):
        a[k] = a[k - 1] * float((2 * k - 1)) / float((2 * k))
    return a


def _make_initial_candidates(n, rng):
    """Return a few plausible starting sequences."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return [np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)]

    cands = []
    r = _make_sqrt_series(n)
    cands.append(r)
    cands.append(r[::-1].copy())

    if n > 1:
        t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)
        cands.append(r * (1.0 + 0.30 * t))
        cands.append(r * (1.0 - 0.20 * t))

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5
    cands.append(np.power(x, -0.50))
    cands.append(np.power(x, -0.45))
    cands.append(r * np.exp(0.20 * rng.standard_normal(n)))
    return cands


def _conv_fft(a, size):
    """Linear convolution a*a via FFT."""
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    C = A * A
    out = np.fft.irfft(C, size)
    return out[:2 * int(a.size) - 1]


def _score_from_c(a, c):
    """Exact objective from a and its convolution coefficients."""
    if a.size == 0 or c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    S = float(a.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    S2 = S * S
    if not np.isfinite(S2) or S2 <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax):
        return np.inf
    if cmax <= 0.0:
        cmax = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * a.size * cmax / S2)


def _local_score(a):
    """Local FFT-based objective, normalized to sqrt(2n) sum."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return np.inf
    S = float(a.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(S) or S <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    target = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * a.size))
    a = a * (target / S)
    csize = _next_p2(2 * int(a.size) - 1)
    c = _conv_fft(a, csize)
    return _score_from_c(a, c)


def _choose_best_initial(n, rng):
    """Choose among a few initial shapes and return the best by local score."""
    best = None
    best_score = np.inf
    for cand in _make_initial_candidates(n, rng):
        sc = _local_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = np.asarray(cand, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if best is None:
        best = _make_sqrt_series(n)
        best_score = _local_score(best)
    return best, best_score


def _get_height_sequence():
    """Safely retrieve the optional global height_sequence_1."""
    try:
        h = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return None
    # Very long stored sequences may be too slow for the final evaluator.
    if arr.size > 12000:
        return None
    return arr


def _power_weights(c, q):
    """
    Smooth peak weights p_k proportional to (max-c ratio)^q.
    p sums to 1 and is nonnegative.
    """
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    L = int(c.size)
    if L == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 0.0:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
    scaled = c / cmax
    q = max(1, int(q))
    w = scaled ** q
    ws = float(w.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        idx = int(np.argmax(c))
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w
    w /= ws
    return w


def _gradient(a, weights, grad_size, allow_negative=False):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k weights[k] * (a*a)[k] with respect to a.

    For J = sum_k w_k c_k:
        dJ/da_m = 2 * sum_j a_j w_{m+j}.

    Computed as a real convolution with reversed weights.
    """
    n = int(a.size)
    L = int(weights.size)
    rw = weights[::-1]
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, grad_size)
    P = np.fft.rfft(rw, grad_size)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(A * P, grad_size)

    start = L - 1
    stop = start - n
    r = conv[start:stop:-1]
    if r.size != n:
        # Fallback for unusual length interactions.
        r = conv[max(0, L - n):L]
        r = r[-n:]

    g = 2.0 * r
    if not allow_negative:
        g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    return g


def _optimize_fixed_mode(a_init, max_seconds, mode, rng, label=""):
    """
    Optimize for one mode.

    mode = -1 : fast flattening direction from L2 residual c - mean(c)
    mode > 0  : peak subgradient with weights (c/max(c))**mode
    """
    a = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        if a.ndim == 1 and a.size > 0:
            n = int(a.size)
        else:
            n = 4096
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n = int(a.size)
    target = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    S = float(a.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(S) or S <= 1e-12:
        a = np.full(n, target / n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a * (target / S)

    z = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    z = _center_clip_z(z)
    a = _softmax_target(z, target)

    conv_size = _next_p2(2 * n - 1)
    grad_size = _next_p2(3 * n - 2)

    c = _conv_fft(a, conv_size)
    score = _score_from_c(a, c)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score
    best_z = z.copy()

    if max_seconds <= 0.0:
        return best_a, best_score

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(max_seconds)

    step = 0.25 if mode == -1 else 0.40
    fail = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = 0.0

    if mode == -1:
        print(f"[{label}] mode=L2 n={n} init={score:.9f}")
    else:
        print(f"[{label}] mode=q{mode} n={n} init={score:.9f}")

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        # Build a descent direction.
        if mode == -1:
            S = float(a.sum(dtype=np.float64))
            y = (S * S) / float(max(1, 2 * n - 1))
            w = c - y
            w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0.0)
            g = _gradient(a, w, grad_size, allow_negative=True)
            scale = float(np.max(np.abs(g))) if g.size else 0.0
        else:
            p = _power_weights(c, mode)
            g = _gradient(a, p, grad_size, allow_negative=False)
            scale = float(np.max(g)) if g.size else 0.0

        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-300:
            break

        d = g / scale

        if fail > 40:
            # Reset to the best seen in this phase, with a modest random kick.
            noise = 0.03 + 0.02 * min(1.0, float(fail) / 100.0)
            z = best_z.copy() + noise * rng.standard_normal(n)
            z = _center_clip_z(z)
            a = _softmax_target(z, target)
            c = _conv_fft(a, conv_size)
            score = _score_from_c(a, c)
            step = 0.25 if mode == -1 else 0.40
            fail = 0
            continue

        if mode == -1:
            factors = (0.60, 0.90, 1.20)
        else:
            factors = (0.50, 1.00, 2.00)

        best_cand_score = score
        best_cand = None
        chosen_step = step

        for fac in factors:
            st = step * float(fac)
            if st <= 0.0:
                continue
            zt = z - st * d
            # Occasionally add a tiny perturbation while not improving.
            if fail > 12 and fac == 1.0 and rng.random() < 0.15:
                zt = zt + 0.02 * rng.standard_normal(n)
            zt = _center_clip_z(zt)

            at = _softmax_target(zt, target)
            ct = _conv_fft(at, conv_size)
            sc = _score_from_c(at, ct)

            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score:
                best_cand_score = sc
                best_cand = (zt, at, ct)
                chosen_step = st

        if best_cand is not None and best_cand_score < score - 1e-12:
            z, a, c = best_cand
            score = best_cand_score
            step = min(2.0, max(0.02, chosen_step * 1.08))
            fail = 0

            if score < best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_z = z.copy()
                now = time.monotonic()
                if now - last_print > 5.0:
                    print(f"[{label}] improved best={score:.9f} after {it} iters")
                    last_print = now
        else:
            step *= 0.70
            fail += 1
            if step < 0.015:
                step = 0.25 if mode == -1 else 0.40

    return best_a, best_score


def _run_job(a_init, max_seconds, mode_schedule, rng, label, hard_deadline, baseline_score):
    """
    Run several phases on the same job, keeping only improvements.

    mode_schedule: list of (mode, fraction), e.g. [(-1, 0.2), (8, 0.5), (32, 0.3)]
    """
    seq = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_score = baseline_score

    for mode, frac in mode_schedule:
        t = float(max_seconds) * float(frac)
        t = min(t, float(hard_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if t <= 1.0:
            continue
        seq_new, score_new = _optimize_fixed_mode(seq, t, int(mode), rng, label)
        if np.isfinite(score_new) and score_new < best_score:
            best_score = score_new
            seq = np.asarray(seq_new, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    return seq, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=400, **kwargs):
    """Search for a low-order-convolution nonnegative step-height sequence."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    now = time.monotonic()
    allowed = float(budget_s) - 3.0
    if allowed < 1.0:
        allowed = 1.0
    hard_deadline = now + allowed

    # Safe fallback: the square-root series at length 4096.
    global_best = _make_sqrt_series(4096)
    global_score = _local_score(global_best)
    if not np.isfinite(global_score):
        global_best = np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64)
        global_score = 2.0

    try:
        print(f"budget={float(budget_s):.2f}s, initial fallback score={global_score:.9f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Optional stored SOTA sequence.
    height = _get_height_sequence()
    height_score = np.inf
    if height is not None:
        height_score = _local_score(height)
        if np.isfinite(height_score):
            print(f"height_sequence_1 length={height.size}, local score={height_score:.9f}")

    # Choose strong starts.
    small_n = 4096
    big_n = 8192 if float(budget_s) > 90 else None

    seq_small, score_small = _choose_best_initial(small_n, rng)
    print(f"n={small_n} best initial local score={score_small:.9f}")

    seq_big = None
    score_big = np.inf
    if big_n is not None:
        seq_big, score_big = _choose_best_initial(big_n, rng)
        print(f"n={big_n} best initial local score={score_big:.9f}")

    # Build job list with time fractions.
    job_list = []  # (sequence, fraction, baseline_score, label)

    if np.isfinite(height_score) and height is not None:
        if height_score < 1.52:
            hf = 0.45
        elif height_score < 1.60:
            hf = 0.35
        elif height_score < 1.72:
            hf = 0.18
        else:
            hf = 0.0
        if hf > 0.0:
            job_list.append((height, hf, height_score, "height"))

    rest = 1.0
    if job_list:
        rest -= sum(float(f) for (_, f, _, _) in job_list)
    if rest < 0.0:
        rest = 0.0

    if seq_small is not None:
        if rest <= 0.0:
            f_small = 0.0
            f_big = 0.0
        elif seq_big is not None:
            f_big = 0.65 * rest
            f_small = rest - f_big
        else:
            f_small = rest
            f_big = 0.0

        if f_small > 0.01:
            job_list.append((seq_small, f_small, score_small, "n4096"))

    if seq_big is not None:
        f_big = 0.0
        if job_list:
            used = sum(float(f) for (_, f, _, _) in job_list)
            f_big = max(0.0, 1.0 - used)
            # If height took a large fraction, still leave reasonable time for big n.
            # The barycentric scheme above already deducts height.
        if f_big > 0.01:
            # Explicitly compute if not already set.
            f_big = 0.0
            if not job_list:
                f_big = 1.0
            else:
                used = sum(float(f) for (_, f, _, _) in job_list)
                f_big = max(0.0, 1.0 - used)
            if f_big > 0.01:
                job_list.append((seq_big, f_big, score_big, "n8192"))

    # If no jobs were created (e.g. extremely tiny budget), keep fallback.
    # Process jobs in order of current baseline score (best first).
    job_list.sort(key=lambda x: (x[2] if np.isfinite(x[2]) else np.inf))

    total_budget = max(1.0, float(hard_deadline - time.monotonic()))
    default_schedule = [(-1, 0.15), (8, 0.35), (32, 0.30), (64, 0.20)]

    for seq, frac, base_score, label in job_list:
        t = float(total_budget) * float(frac)
        t = min(t, float(hard_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if t <= 2.0 or seq is None:
            continue
        seq2, score2 = _run_job(
            seq, t, default_schedule, rng, label, float(hard_deadline), float(base_score)
        )
        if np.isfinite(score2) and score2 < global_score:
            global_score = score2
            global_best = np.asarray(seq2, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"global update from {label}: {global_score:.9f}")

    # Final polish if time remains.
    remaining = float(hard_deadline - time.monotonic())
    if remaining > 15.0 and global_best is not None:
        polish_schedule = [(-1, 0.20), (16, 0.45), (64, 0.35)]
        seq3, score3 = _run_job(
            global_best, remaining, polish_schedule, rng,
            "polish", float(hard_deadline), float(global_score)
        )
        if np.isfinite(score3) and score3 < global_score:
            global_score = score3
            global_best = np.asarray(seq3, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"polished global: {global_score:.9f}")

    # Clean final sequence.
    out = np.asarray(global_best, dtype=np.float64)
    if out.ndim != 1 or out.size == 0:
        out = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(out.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(S) or S <= 1e-12:
        out = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
        S = float(out.sum(dtype=np.float64))

    # Scale to sum sqrt(2n) for numerical stability in the exact evaluator.
    target = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * out.size))
    if S > 0.0 and np.isfinite(S):
        out = out * (target / S)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)

    try:
        print(f"final length={out.size}, final local score={global_score:.9f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
